                    CHIRAL KOSZUL DUALITY
  NONCOMMUTATIVE FACTORISATION D-MODULES,
ORDERED QUANTUM FIELDS, AND THE GEOMETRY OF
                 DEFECTS
  E1 -algebras in factorisable D-modules on ordered chiral configurations

                                        RAEEZ LORGAT

                                             July 2026


                                              Abstract
     The primitive object of this paper is an augmented E1 -algebra in the symmetric monoidal
 category of factorisable right D-modules on a smooth complex curve X. We call it a noncommutative
 ordered chiral algebra. It carries two independent local geometries. The factorisation D-module
 contains the complete singular dependence of fields on X, including every principal part and its
 covariance under coordinate change. The internal E1 multiplication records associative, generally
 noncommutative fusion in a transverse oriented line. In the regular-holonomic analytic realization,
 the same local structure is a product-generated factorisation algebra on X an ×R that is holomorphic
 in X and locally constant along R.
     The Ran category carries two compatible monoidal products: pointwise tensor and disjoint-union
 chiral convolution. Their Beck–Chevalley interchange makes the relevant subcategory duoidal. A
 noncommutative ordered chiral algebra is equivalently a bimonoid whose pointwise multiplication is
 a morphism of factorisation coalgebras. Locality is therefore a bialgebra law between two different
 products. Its ordered bar inherits a coassociative deconcatenation coproduct and a commutative
 chiral factorisation coproduct, and the two coproducts commute by the same interchange law.
     The natural indexing object is the operadic Grothendieck construction for E1 ⊗ ChX . It records
 an ordered sequence of entire finite chiral configurations, not a linear order on the points of
 one configuration. Straightening identifies its factorisation objects with internal E1 -algebras. A
 Boardman–Vogt resolution is indexed by planar levelled forests with two edge colours, one for
 transverse composition and one for chiral collision. In every oriented cellular realization, the two
 edge-contraction differentials anticommute because their determinant lines are independent.
     The ordered chiral bar construction is the ordinary simplicial bar performed in the coefficient
 category:                                                      Z
                        Barord
                           X (A) = [r] 7−→ 1 ⊗ Ā
                                                  ⊗r
                                                     ⊗1 ≃                        A.
                                                                ([0,1],∂[0,1])

 Its face maps fuse adjacent transverse factors. The full chiral operator product remains in the
 coefficient D-module; no logarithmic residue is asked to recover a double, fourth, or arbitrary pole.
 The associahedral bar is a cofibrant model of this derived interval tensor product, not a termwise
 replacement of the simplicial bar. On connected positive-weight objects, and on their complete
 Mittag–Leffler limits, the ordinary associative bar–cobar theorem applies internally and reconstructs
 A. Separately, the cardinality filtration is pro-nilpotent for the chiral convolution product on
 D(Ran X), where the enhanced Francis–Gaitsgory bar–cobar equivalence holds. The two theorems
 have different monoidal products and are related only by a specified monoidal comparison.
R. Lorgat                                                           Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


        The boundary Koszul dual
                                             A! := RHomA (1, 1)
     is the endomorphism algebra of the augmentation boundary condition, and the double dual is the
     derived augmentation completion for positive finite-type objects. If C = Barord  X (A), the universal
     twisting morphism gives an equivalence between augmentation-complete A-modules and conilpotent
     C-comodules. Its two-sided form sends the diagonal bimodule to the diagonal bicomodule C∆ ,
     carries derived tensor product to derived cotensor product, and identifies Hochschild theory with
     coHochschild theory. Thus the interval, the annulus, and the derived centre form one Koszul–Morita
     square. Quadratic Koszul duality, PBW deformation, curved nonhomogeneous duality, and Verdier
     duality are comparison constructions to this universal bar resolution; none is identified with another
     by notation.
         Order does not contain exchange. Each chamber t1 < · · · < tn of Conf n (R) is contractible, so a
     bare E1 algebra has no canonical braid operator. Exchange appears after an E2 enhancement, a
     flat connection on a two-dimensional configuration space, or equivalent meromorphic tensor data.
     The internal Hochschild cochains

                                       Zord,ch (A) = RHomA⊗Aop (A, A)

     form an E2 algebra in factorisable D-modules and are the universal recipient of central actions.
     A rigid meromorphic line category with a faithful strong monoidal fibre functor reconstructs
     a coquasitriangular bialgebra, and a Hopf algebra when rigidity and the relevant continuous
     duals supply an antipode. A faithful quasi-monoidal fibre functor reconstructs instead a coquasi-
     bialgebra, or a quasi-Hopf algebra under the corresponding rigidity hypotheses. Yangians, quantum
     loop algebras, and elliptic quantum groups enter only through additional RTT, monodromy,
     or representation-theoretic recognition theorems. Knizhnik–Zamolodchikov transport and four-
     dimensional Chern–Simons theory provide exact examples of this passage.
         A square-zero BRST derivation extends factorwise to the ordered bar and anticommutes with its
     bar differential. The resulting spectral sequence compares the bar of the derived BRST reduction
     with BRST cohomology of the bar. Quantum Drinfeld–Sokolov reduction is treated in this form;
     passage to an ordinary degree-zero W -algebra is made only under a cohomological concentration
     theorem.
         There are two genera. A Calabi–Yau trace on the transverse E1 algebra closes intervals into
     circles and produces Hochschild chains, annuli, ribbon graphs, and open topological surfaces. A
     modular sewing structure on the chiral coefficient glues pointed algebraic curves and produces
     conformal blocks over Deligne–Mumford moduli. We encode their simultaneous formal algebra by
     a two-edge-coloured Feynman transform. Its master equation is
                               1         1
                           dΘ + [Θ, Θ]X + [Θ, Θ]⊥ + ℏX ∆X Θ + ℏ⊥ ∆⊥ Θ = 0,
                               2         2
     where the two loop contractions have different geometric sources and supercommute. Ribbon genus
     and chiral genus may be related by a physical duality, but they are not equal by definition. Curved
     Koszul data, determinant-line curvature, BRST anomaly, framing anomaly, and conformal anomaly
     likewise remain in their native deformation complexes until an explicit comparison identifies them.
         The mathematical and physical descriptions meet through factorisation observables. A per-
     turbatively renormalised holomorphic–topological BV theory on X × R whose quantum master
     equation holds determines an ordered chiral algebra. Compactified configuration spaces resolve
     ultraviolet collisions; factorisation and the BV master equation identify the boundary restrictions
     of renormalised graph weights; a second topological direction supplies line exchange and the
     Yang–Baxter equation. The derived centre is the universal algebraic target of the bulk action,
     while equality with a physical bulk is a separate quasi-isomorphism theorem.
         The paper develops this language through diagonals, pole filtrations, partition lattices, Fulton–


                                                       2
R. Lorgat                                                         Chiral Koszul duality for ordered fields


     MacPherson screens, Ran spaces, ordered intervals, Hochschild and Chevalley constructions, surface
     braid groups, ribbon surfaces, stable curves, and determinant lines. The constructions are examined
     on tensor, quiver, matrix, Weyl, Clifford, and enveloping chiral algebras; Heisenberg and affine
     currents; βγ and bc systems; Virasoro and principal W -algebras; KZ and KZB connections;
     Yangians; conformal blocks and Verlinde traces; Chern–Simons boundary algebras; Calabi–Yau
     pushforwards; Hall products; and Borcherds automorphic products, always through the comparison
     theorem native to the example. Every numerical quantity appears only after a specified trace,
     index, monodromy, or comparison morphism has been constructed.
